2026. 10.27
先週（10/19～10/26）発表の主な経済指標
米生産活動は堅調。AI関連投資を背景にコンピューター・電子
製品や機械などがけん引。一方、データセンター建設に伴う需要
増加が、他産業の生産や建設活動を圧迫する兆候も
コア消費者物価は再び2％超え。特殊要因を除くサービス価格
も加速し、人件費転嫁の兆し。連合は2027年春闘で6％中心
を要求。中小企業の余力に課題も、物価上振れに注意
注目点② サービス物価の加速で重み増す2027年春闘戦線注目点①AI投資の恩恵拡大、副作用もじわり顕在化
総合PMIは57.6と高水準を維持。新規失業保険申
請件数も19.4万件と低水準で解雇の動きは引き続
き限定的。一方で、住宅着工件数や住宅建設業
者の景況感は悪化、高金利の影響が継続

耐久財製造業は、軍需やデータセンター（DC）増
設の旺盛な需要が、他の製品の生産を圧迫して
いると報告ベージュブック
（2026/10） テクノロジー企業がDC向け電気設備の買い占め
を試みたが、供給業者は他案件への供給を優先
し、これを拒否
機械製造業は、DC向けに鋼材在庫が大量に消
費されるなか、鉄鋼メーカーによる供給制限が他
の建設案件の遅延を招いていると指摘
テキサス州
見通し調査
（2026/9）
製造業では原材料コストが上昇。特に鋼材価格
が急上昇しており、その一因はDC需要とされるベージュブック
（2026/9） 一部の製造業がアルミニウム・銅・鋼材の供給不
足や調達期間の長期化を報告
一部の自動車メーカーはアルミニウムや一部部品
の供給不足で生産が幾分抑制されたと報告

ベージュブック
（2026/7）
米国：AI関連を中心に製造業生産は堅調も、資材・設備の需給ひっ迫が他産業に波及
 米国の製造業生産は、AI関連投資を背景にコンピューター・電子製品、一次金属、一般機械などが堅調
― 一方、非耐久財や家具など消費関連の生産は軟調に推移し、業種間のばらつきが拡大
 FRBは、データセンター建設に伴う鋼材・電気設備などの需要増加が、他の建設案件や生産活動に影響していると指摘
―AI投資の拡大が関連産業の生産を押し上げる一方、供給制約を通じて他産業の活動を圧迫する兆候も
― 当面はAI投資による成長押し上げ効果が期待される一方、供給制約や価格上昇を通じた負の影響にも注意が必要
4

日本：全国コアCPIは再び2％超え。基調的なサービス価格も伸び高まる
5
 9月の全国コアCPIは前年比＋2.1％（前月：同＋1.7％）と加速。2025年12月以来の2％超に
― 原油高・円安等のコスト上昇もあり、家事用消耗品や教養娯楽用品等を中心に伸び率が拡大
― 一般サービス価格も伸び加速。シルバーウィークの並びによる宿泊料や外食費の上昇などが主因
 特殊要因を除いた一般サービス価格もじわり上昇
― 変動の大きい宿泊費や私立高校無償化による押し下げを除いた基調的な一般サービス価格に上昇圧力。人件費増の
価格転嫁が進んでいる可能性を示唆

全国コアCPI CPI一般サービス価格の推移

### Page 7

日本：連合は2027年春闘で賃上げ要求を強化。高水準の賃上げ持続と物価への波及が焦点
6
 連合は、2027年春闘賃上げ要求の目安を「6％中心、5％以上」と発表
―5％以上の賃上げを最低目標として明確化。実質賃金1％上昇の定着をめざす方針
― 中小企業は大企業との格差是正に向け「6％以上」が目標。過去4年のインフレで実質賃金が低下した分の回復も要求
― また、新たに、2030年に2026年の賃金水準を15％程度上回る中期目標を設定。持続的な賃金上昇をめざす
 賃上げ余力は企業規模・業種で濃淡。中小企業等で高い「賃上げ5％の壁」
― なお、6％に近い賃上げが実現する場合、サービス価格への波及が強まり、基調的物価上昇率が2％を上回る可能性
連合・春闘基本構想の新旧比較 春闘賃上げ率

基本構想」 より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 8

欧州：2027年度予算審議が本格化。生活苦によるデモが拡大する中、財政赤字は高水準
7
 2027年度予算審議が本格化。財政赤字は総じて高止まり
― 一定の防衛費・エネルギー関連支出を柔軟化する特例措置の適用後も、複数の国でEU財政基準で定める赤字幅を超
過。スペインは予算案を巡って議会解散。フランス・ベルギーも10/15時点でEUに予算案を未提出
 生活改善を求める抗議活動が欧州各国で発生。支出圧力の高まりは財政再建の障壁に
― フランスの学生によるデモを発端に、生活費の高騰や公共サービスの劣化に対する抗議活動は欧州各国に拡大。一部
は暴動に発展し、警察官と衝突する事態に
9～10月に発生した主な抗議活動主要国の財政収支実績と見通し
（出所） 各種報道より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

欧州：金融市場は政治・財政を巡る混乱を嫌気。不透明感は2027年入り後も継続する見込み
8
 金融市場は政治・財政を巡る混乱を警戒。主要国の選挙を控える中、不透明感は2027年まで継続へ
― 抗議活動が広がるイタリア、フランス、ベルギーの一般政府債務残高（GDP比）は、EU加盟国の中でも上位（それぞれ2
位、3位、4位）。金利上昇の背景は政治・財政への懸念
― 影響は為替市場にも波及。9月中旬以降、ユーロはドルに対して弱含み
―2027年にかけてフランス大統領選やスペイン・イタリアの総選挙など、欧州主要国で重要な国政選挙が相次ぐ。極右・ポ

美期中選舉牽動市場表現，股債配置重品質、控風險
2026 年 11 月 04 日
一、 市場動態
1. 美期中選舉牽動市場情緒，亞股一度隨美股期貨下挫：美國 11 月 3 日舉行期中選舉
，根據 CNN 網站的結果顯示，截至台北時間 11/4 下午 3 點左右，民主黨於參議院
已取得 100 席中的 53 席的過半席次，於眾議院則在 435 席中取得了 225 席(218 席
過半)。市場重新評估國會控制權及川普政府未來政策推動能力，擔心民主黨國會影響
力擴大，可能對財政政策及科技企業監管形成新的制衡。S&P 500 期貨與 Nasdaq 100

期貨在開票期間一度下挫 1.38%及 2.35%，科技股明顯承受較大賣壓。惟隨後跌深反
彈，截至台北時間下午 3 時左右，S&P 500 期貨與 Nasdaq 100 期貨跌幅已收斂至
0.22%及 0.44%。亞洲市場方面，科技股為主及出口導向股市受到美股期貨拖累，台
灣加權指數 11/4 盤中最低下挫 3.38%，所幸隨跌幅收斂，終場下跌 0.83%；韓股同
日也由大跌近 4%由黑翻紅上漲 0.62%。11/3 未開盤的日股，在 11/4 開盤後出現補
跌，最低下挫近 4.8%，但最後跌幅收斂至下跌 1.42%。
2. 美國公債及美元同步反映政策預期調整，市場避險情緒升溫：美國公債及美元反映政
策預期調整，殖利率盤中先跌後漲：10 年期公債殖利率盤中先跌後漲，截至台北時間
下午 3 時來到 4.906%；同一時間，美元指數小跌約 0.37%。股、債、匯市的變動顯
示，投資人除了調整股票風險部位，也在評估未來美國財政刺激規模、政府債務供給
與利率前景。
二、 下跌原因
1. 民主黨取得國會控制權，市場重新定價財政與監管政策：本次選舉對金融市場的主要

影響，在於未來行政與立法部門的權力分配可能改變。共和黨先前同時掌握白宮與國
會，有利於推動稅制、財政及產業政策；民主黨在參眾兩院均取得過半席次後，部分

### Page 2

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
政策將面臨更高的協商成本。財政方面，新增減稅及擴大支出的空間可能受到限制，
市場因而下修部分財政刺激預期；企業方面，國會監督權可能增加對大型科技企業、
AI 資料中心、反壟斷及資料隱私議題的調查壓力。惟國會控制權改變不代表既有政策
立即逆轉，也不代表所有監管措施均能完成立法。此次市場反應更可能是投資人重新
要求政策風險溢酬，而非企業獲利已發生實質下修。
2. AI 資本支出與投資回報疑慮尚未消退，放大科技股對政治事件的反應：此次選舉開票
期間，Nasdaq 期貨及與科技相關的亞股出現大幅波動，亦與近期科技股基本面及評

價爭議有關。10 月底以來美股因大型科技企業 AI 投資效益受到質疑而大幅修正，市
場關注焦點逐漸由 AI 基礎建設支出規模，轉向相關投資能否創造足夠的營運現金流與
股東報酬。以雲端服務及資料中心產業而言，企業雖可透過 AI 運算需求增加帶動營收
成長，但 GPU、伺服器、電力設備及資料中心建設需要龐大前期資金，若營收回收速
度不如預期，可能使自由現金流承壓並增加外部融資需求。近期部分科技企業籌資計
畫及財測不佳，已使投資人提高對財務品質的要求。當市場原先已對 AI 投資回報存有
疑慮，新的政治及監管不確定性便更容易放大評價修正幅度。
三、 市場看法
1. 半導體需求仍具分化，政治事件不等於 AI 產業成長趨勢反轉：科技股近期的股價修正
與 AI 終端需求惡化並非相同概念。AMD 近期公布優於市場預期的財報，並對資料中
心業務提出較強勁的成長展望，顯示 AI 運算需求仍具有一定韌性；相對而言，Intel
先前財測疲弱及現金流壓力，則反映不同半導體企業的產品競爭力、資本效率及終端
市場曝險存在明顯差異。因此，不宜將半導體股整體下跌解讀為 AI 需求全面降溫。從

產業鏈觀察，具備技術領先優勢、穩定客戶需求及良好現金流的企業，與高度依賴外
部融資、尚未形成穩定獲利模式的基礎建設業者，對資金成本及監管變化的承受能力
並不相同。未來市場可能更加重視個別企業的獲利品質，而非單純以 AI 相關營收成長
作為估值依據。
2. 政治事件可能延長科技股評價調整，但中期走勢仍取決於企業獲利：本次期中選舉引
發的期貨跌勢，反映市場對政策方向及國會控制權變化的即時反應，惟政治不確定性
通常須透過實際立法、企業投資計畫或經濟活動變化，才會形成較持久的基本面影響
。從企業獲利角度觀察，路透社引用 LSEG 資料指出，S&P 500 第三季企業獲利仍呈
現強勁成長，科技企業對整體獲利貢獻顯著，顯示目前尚無充分證據支持美國企業基
本面全面轉弱。不過，科技股先前已累積較高成長預期，市場對資本支出回報及監管
風險的容忍度降低，可能使股價即使在營收成長下仍承受評價壓力。短期而言，若選
舉結果逐漸明朗，且企業獲利預估未進一步下修，市場有機會逐步消化政治風險；反
之，若政策不確定性伴隨獲利預期下修，修正時間可能延長。

策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
3. 歷史經驗顯示選後股市多正向，後續表現仍宜留意基本面變化：根據彭博統計，1950
年以來美國歷次期中選舉後 12 個月，S&P 500 平均報酬率約 15%，且 19 次全數
收正。其中最高為 1954 年的逾 30%，表現最弱的 1986 年亦小幅上漲。我們認為
，選前政策不確定性消除加上府會分治抑制財政與監管的轉向是為歷史上選後行情的
主要支撐，但相關數據所呈現的為過往經驗，未來股市表現能否延續先前情況仍應觀
察後續基本面變化。
4. 美國公債具避險價值，但利率下行能否持續仍取決於通膨與財政前景：在美國期中選

舉開票期間，美股期貨下跌伴隨美債殖利率回落，顯示公債仍能在股市波動期間提供
一定的風險分散效果。然而，本次殖利率下降可能同時包含避險買盤、財政刺激預期
下修及市場對經濟成長前景重新評估等因素，不宜直接解讀為聯準會即將轉向寬鬆。
尤其能源市場仍受到中東地緣政治及供應中斷風險影響，油價波動可能使通膨前景反
覆，限制長天期公債殖利率的下行空間。若未來財政擴張受到限制，可能對長端利率
形成部分支撐；但若政治僵局提高債務上限協商風險，亦可能使美債期限溢酬波動。
因此，債券配置仍須同時考量收益率、存續期間與通膨風險，不宜僅根據單日行情決
定配置方向。
5. 信用市場是判斷風險是否由股權評價擴散至金融條件的重要指標：目前科技股修正主
要反映評價與政策風險，但若 AI 企業持續增加資本支出及外部融資，市場對債務負擔
與償債能力的關注可能進一步升高。對大型投資等級科技企業而言，較穩定的營運現
金流及融資管道有助於吸收資本支出壓力；但對依賴單一客戶合約、設備抵押融資或
高槓桿經營的 AI 基礎建設業者，利率與信用利差上升可能更直接影響投資回報及再融

資能力。後續應追蹤投資等級債與高收益債 OAS、科技及雲端相關發行人利差、新債
發行需求及信用評等展望。若科技股下跌但整體信用利差維持穩定，市場風險可能仍
以股權評價調整為主；若信用利差同步持續擴大，則須提高對金融條件收緊及風險跨
市場擴散的警覺。

四、 投資建議：
1. 科技股中期成長趨勢仍具支撐，惟短線宜審慎布局並重視個股分化：美國期中選舉帶
來的政策不確定性，短期可能使科技股評價承壓，惟大型雲端業者持續投入 AI 基礎建
設，半導體及相關設備需求仍具中期成長動能，目前尚不宜因美股期貨在開票期間的
下挫即全面轉向保守。我們仍建議維持美股核心配置，但短期避免過度集中於高評價
、高資本支出及高度依賴外部融資的企業。產業方面，資訊科技維持選擇性配置，優
先關注具備技術競爭優勢、穩定營運現金流及較高投資回報能見度的企業；對雲端基
礎建設及 AI 融資相關標的，則應提高財務結構與現金流審查標準。

2. 維持中天期美國公債及高信評投資等級債配置，兼顧收益與風險分散：在美國政策風
險升溫及科技股評價調整期間，美國公債仍具投資組合避險功能，但考量通膨與能源
價格的不確定性，現階段不建議延長存續期間。建議以中天期美國公債及高信評投資
等級債作為主要配置方向，兼顧票息收益、利率敏感度與資產配置穩定性。信用債方
面，建議優先選擇營運現金流充足、槓桿適中及再融資需求可控的發行人，對 AI 相關
企業則須進一步評估資本支出、合約現金流與債務到期結構。

价格走势分化，PPI 涨幅扩大
本月 CPI 同比环比均有所回落，食品价格是主要的下降项，而上涨项
仍然是汽油为主的能源价格。汽油价格同比上涨 22.4%，涨幅比上月
扩大 5.8个百分点，影响 CPI同比上涨约 0.63个百分点，带动能源价
格涨幅由上月的 7.3%扩大至本月的 9.9%。扣除食品和能源价格的核
心 CPI 同比上涨 1.0%，小幅回升，基本保持稳定。 PPI 同比上涨
5.7%，涨幅比上月扩大 0.7个百分点。价格上涨的主要行业有，煤炭
开采、石油开采、石油加工、化学制品、燃气、计算机通信、有色金
属矿采选、有色金属冶炼、电气机械，上述 9个行业合计影响 PPI 同
比上涨约 6.14个百分点，上拉影响比上月增加 0.64个百分点。

1 事件：10月 CPI、PPI数据公布
国家统计局数据显示，2026年 10月份，全国居民消费价格同
比上涨 0.7%。其中，城市上涨 0.7%，农村上涨 0.7%；食品价格
下降 2.9%，非食品价格上涨 1.5%；消费品价格上涨 0.8%，服务
价格上涨 0.7%。1—10月平均，全国居民消费价格比上年同期上
涨 0.9%。10月份，全国居民消费价格环比持平。其中，城市持平，
农村下降 0.1%；食品价格下降 1.0%，非食品价格上涨 0.2%；消
费品价格下降 0.1%，服务价格上涨 0.1%。
2026年 10月份，全国工业生产者出厂价格同比上涨 5.7%，
环比上涨 0.7%。工业生产者购进价格同比上涨 7.9%，环比上涨
1.1%。1—10月平均，工业生产者出厂价格比上年同期上涨 2.7%，
工业生产者购进价格上涨 4.0%。
表 1：10月 CPI、PPI同比数据（%）
资料来源：IFIND，上海证券研究所
2 事件解析：数据特征和变动原因

2.1 CPI小幅回落
本月全国 CPI环比由上月上涨 0.2%转为持平。食品价格由上
月上涨 0.4%转为下降 1.0%，影响 CPI环比下降约 0.17个百分点，
是 CPI 环比下降的主要原因。其中，猪肉、鲜菜、鸡蛋、鲜果、
水产品价格均有不同程度的下降。非食品方面，受国际市场价格
变动影响，国内汽油价格上涨 4.2%，涨幅比上月回落 0.7 个百分
点，影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.14个百分点；服务方面，服务价格由
上月下降 0.2%转为上涨 0.1%，影响 CPI 环比上涨约 0.04个百分
点，其中受国庆长假的刺激，相关旅游出行等服务价格有所上涨。
CPI 同比上涨 0.7%，涨幅比上月回落 0.2 个百分点。具体来
看，食品价格下降 2.9%，降幅比上月扩大 1.2 个百分点，影响
CPI 同比下降约 0.49 个百分点。食品中，鲜菜、猪肉和鲜果价格
分别下降 16.9%、8.6%和 3.8%，合计影响 CPI 同比下降约 0.54
个百分点。非食品方面，受汽油涨价影响，能源价格涨幅扩大。

回落 0.2个百分点，基本保持稳定。
同比变动从主要食品分类来看，除猪肉、粮食外均较上月均
有所下降。鲜菜价格下降 16.9%，影响 CPI下降约 0.31个百分点；
鲜果价格下降 3.8%，影响 CPI 下降约 0.07 个百分点；畜肉类价
格下降 2.7%，影响 CPI 下降约 0.11个百分点，其中猪肉价格下
降 8.6%；蛋类价格上涨 10.1%，影响 CPI上涨约 0.05个百分点

工、燃气等行业价格均有不同程度上涨，合计影响 PPI 环比上涨
约 0.61个百分点，超过总涨幅的八成。

点。价格上涨的主要行业中，煤炭开采和洗选业上涨 36.6%，石

扩大 8.3 个百分点；黑色金属冶炼和压延加工业上涨 0.4%，由负
转正。
3 事件影响：对经济和市场
3.1 价格走势分化，PPI涨幅扩大
图 5：生产资料和生活资料价格同比（%）

### Page 8

宏观数据
请务必阅读尾页重要声明 8
本月 CPI 同比环比均有所回落，食品价格是主要的下降项，
而上涨项仍然是汽油为主的能源价格。汽油价格同比上涨 22.4%，
涨幅比上月扩大 5.8 个百分点，影响 CPI 同比上涨约 0.63 个百分
点，带动能源价格涨幅由上月的 7.3%扩大至本月的 9.9%。扣除
食品和能源价格的核心 CPI 同比上涨 0.8%，涨幅比上月回落 0.2
个百分点，基本保持稳定。PPI 同比上涨 5.7%，涨幅比上月扩大
0.7个百分点。价格上涨的主要行业有，煤炭开采、石油开采、石
油加工、化学制品、燃气、计算机通信、有色金属矿采选、有色
金属冶炼、电气机械，上述 9 个行业合计影响 PPI 同比上涨约
6.14个百分点，上拉影响比上月增加 0.64个百分点。
4 事件预测：趋势判断
4.1 推动经济向新向优向好发展
10月价格持续分化，CPI走势偏稳，PPI持续上涨。CPI同比
的变动越来越受到非食品的影响，尤其是汽油价格，但由于食品
项偏弱的表现，CPI 整体平稳，核心 CPI 也保持稳定。工业品价

格同比涨幅有所扩大，上游原材料与下游制造业均有所涨价，尤
其是人工智能持续赋能产业转型带动计算机通信、电气机械等行
业上涨突出。政策方面，7月政治局会议提出要稳定生猪等农畜产
品生产和价格，实施好更加积极的财政政策和适度宽松的货币政
策，充分发挥各项存量政策效能，及时谋划出台务实管用的增量
政策，加大逆周期调节力度，推动经济持续向新向优向好发展，
努力实现“十五五”良好开局。
5 风险提示：
地缘政治事件恶化，国际金融形势改变，中美政策超预期变
化。

公司簡介 
志聖成立於 1966 年，目前資本額 16.2 億
新台幣，為 G2C 集團當中的核心成員之一，
以紫外光、烘烤、壓膜塗佈、電漿製程等技
術提供相關壓膜、貼、撕膜、烘烤、電鍍相
關設備。公司初期以 PCB 設備起家，而後
伴隨台灣電子產業升級，切入半導體、組裝、
面板、太陽能等產業，近年陸續在 IC 載板、
高階 HDI、先進封裝、HBM 等高附加價值
市場發光發熱。1-3Q26 營收以產業別劃
分，半導體佔約 45%、先進 PCB 28%、傳
統 PCB 21%、其他電子 4%、FPD 3%。

Amkor、Micron、欣興、勝宏等

 投資評價與建議 
維持買進，目標價降至 358 元：(1) 3Q26 獲利表現不如預期，主因 PCB 比重拉升
及面對中國在地價格競爭，(2) 能見度高，但隨企業 AI Server 帶動 HDI/HLC PCB 
與 ABF 載板 2H26-2027 年接力放量，短期獲利改善有限。我們預估 2026/ 2027
年 EPS 為 14.01/19.92 元，目標 PE 則由 30x 下修至 18x，反映市場對設備類股評
價的調整，目標價隨之下修到 358 元(18x 2027 EPSF)。

### Page 2

2

志聖以光與熱為核心，發展熱處理平台：志聖成立於 1966 年，目前資本額 16.2 億
新台幣，為 G2C 集團當中的核心成員之一，以紫外光、烘烤、壓膜塗佈、電漿製
程等技術提供相關壓膜、貼、撕膜、烘烤、電鍍相關設備。公司初期以 PCB 設備
起家，而後伴隨台灣電子產業升級，切入半導體、組裝、面板、太陽能等產業，近
年陸續在 IC 載板、高階 HDI、先進封裝、HBM 等高附加價值市場發光發熱。公司
也與均豪、均華、東捷組成 G2C+聯盟，共同實現台灣先進封裝在地化。志聖主要
生產據點位於台灣林口、台中、中國廣州、昆山，並在台南、廣東、泰國、美國鳳
凰城有服務據點。1-3Q26 營收以產業別劃分，半導體佔約 45%、先進 PCB 28%、
傳統 PCB 21%、其他電子 4%、FPD 3%。主要競爭對手包含 EVG、TOK、SUSS、
Yield Engineering Systems(YES)等。客戶包含台積電、日月光、臻鼎、力成、
Amkor、Micron、欣興、勝宏等電子中上游廠商。 
3Q26 獲利不如預期：志聖 3Q26 營收 29.8 億(+7.0%QoQ，+93.9%YoY)，毛利
率 43.3%，稅後淨利 5.5 億(-13.4%QoQ，+162.1%YoY)，EPS 3.51 元。毛利率
表現不如預期主因出貨較多中國 PCB 用低階設備，使產品組合轉差，即半導體設
備/PCB 設備由 1H26 的 46%/46%變動至 1-3Q26 的 45%/49%，營業費用亦隨
獎酬增加及研發專案數的提升而高於預期。 
能見度高，但我們對獲利趨於審慎：管理層預期 2026 年底在手訂單約 100-110
億，較去年同期約 43 億元倍增，其中半導體佔比約 53%以上(以 OSAT 能見度較
高)、高階 PCB 35-37%。值得注意的是，半導體設備交期已由 4-10 個月拉長至 4-
13 個月；PCB 設備交期則約 3-8 個月，主因 OSAT 在廠房未完工前即下單，以及
上游零組件(機械手臂)交期的拉長所致，志聖持續以 G2C+聯盟和日系供應商溝通
並聯合採購，目前風險可控。展望後市，我們預期在晶圓廠先進封裝產能建置外溢
的環境下，志聖將透過挑單生產，降低傳統 PCB 接單比例以維持獲利率穩健成長。 
載板挖槽嵌入製程，2028 年再添動能：隨 AI 晶片尺寸持續放大，台系載板廠正
發展新的生產方式，以降低整片翹曲同時達到微縮線寬/線距的目的。志聖在此製
程中提供背面貼膜，以及 ABF 絕緣膜的壓合與撕膜設備。由於層數倍增將使壓合
與撕膜次數同步增加，加上載具尺寸放大推升單機價值，我們認為這將是志聖 
2028 年後載板設備的重要成長來源。 
財務預估：研究部預估志聖 2026 年營收 111.1 億元(+82.2%YoY)，毛利率 46.3%，
稅後淨利 22.7 億元(+173.0%YoY)，EPS 14.01 元(原估 16.94 元，下修主因本業
獲利表現不如預期及股本膨脹)。展望 2027 年，公司尚未給予明確展望，我們考量
短期 PCB/HLC 急單湧現壓抑毛利率，故下修獲利預估，預期 2027 年營收 148.4
億元(+33.5%YoY)，毛利率 46.9%，稅後淨利 32.2 億元(+42.2%YoY)，EPS 19.92
元(原估 25.15 元，下修主因獲利預估調降及股本膨脹)。

### Page 1

King Slide

Buy: strong 3Q26 results, 88.2% GM; solid 
’27/28E sales/margin; cut PO to NT$12,988

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 12,988 TWD | Price: 8,165 TWD

3Q26 results: another impressive beat with 88.2% GM 
King Slide’s 3Q26 GM/OPM hit record highs of 88.2%/82.9% for the fourth/second 
consecutive quarter, respectively, well above BofAe of 83.7%/78.2% and consensus of 
84.0%/78.6%. OP (+62% QoQ, +383% YoY) was 7%/5% above BofAe/consensus. We 
attribute the solid margins/results to sizable sales scale (+60% QoQ, +297% YoY) and 
better operating efficiency. With non-operating gain (mainly net interest income), NP 
(+65% QoQ, +266% YoY) was 8%/7% above BofAe/consensus (Exhibit 3). 
BofAe 4Q26 sales -20% QoQ; solid 2027/28 sales/margin 
We now expect King Slide’s 4Q26 sales to decline 20% QoQ, from the high 3Q26 base 
and amid GPU platform transition. We believe Sep/Oct sales (-31%/+2% MoM) shall 
serve as evidence for slower order momentum. Our 4Q26 sales forecast implies flat-to-
slightly higher MoM sales in Nov/Dec. We now expect another 45%/20% YoY sales 
growth in 2027/28, with stronger pull-in demand resuming in 1Q27. In our view, 
2027/28 sales should be supported by various AI projects across platforms (GPU/ASIC/ 
AMD) and clients (global/regional CSPs, neoclouds, AI start-ups, enterprises). We also 
keep monitoring the capacity ramp-up from the new US Houston site, which may bring 
further sales upside. We expect high and sustainable GM at 85.0% both in 2027/28. We 
see FX as a potential swing factor to GM, given the company’s natural-hedging policy.  
Trim PO to NT$12,988: lower P/E set-up; reiterate Buy 
We lower our 2026-27E EPS by 0-4% but lift our 2028E EPS by 7%, mainly to reflect 
3Q26 beat and stronger margins. Yet, we cut PO to NT$12,988 (28x 2027E EPS) from 
NT$14,888 (32x 2027E EPS), with valuation base unchanged but a lower P/E multiple. 
We now use 28x (2SD above average since IPO) on valuation de-rating across AI supply 
chain amid macro uncertainty. Nevertheless, we reiterate Buy on King Slide in view of its 
lion share in AI server rail market, diversified client/project base, strong margin profile.

Company Sector 
IT Hardware

Company Description 
King Slide is the global top server rail kit manufacturer from 
Taiwan. It also supplies slide rail kit for kitchen/furniture. Its 
key server clients include global cloud service providers and 
enterprise servers.

King Slide was founded in 1986 and was publicly listed on 
Taiwan Stock Exchange on 25 June 2008. Its headquarter 
and main production sites are located in Kaohsiung 
(Taiwan). It also has capacity in Houston (US).

Investment Rationale 
We assign a Buy rating to King Slide given its leading 
position in server rail to ride on strong AI market demand 
with rising content value thanks to increasing component 
density and customization. We highlight King Slide's early 
step into server market since 2001 with proven track 
record, much larger scale, time-to-market capability, higher 
automation level, and 3,600+ patents as strong moats for 
high/above-peer margins.

### Page 6

Price objective basis & risk 
King Slide (KGSLF) 
Our PO of NT$12,988 is based on 28x 2027E EPS. We use 28x P/E multiple (2SD above 
P/E average since listing) in view of strong AI server market demand, leading position in 
server rail, solid/high margin profiles, and double-digit sales/EPS CAGR in 2026-28E. Its 
early step into server market since 2001 with proven track record, much larger scale 
than peer, and 3,600+ patents as strong moats for high/above-peer margins.

Upside risks to our PO are: (1) even stronger AI server demand, (2) margin upside thanks 
to larger scale and/or better efficiency, (3) better kitchen slides market demand, (4) 
faster capacity expansion, (5) favorable FX movement (mainly USD/NTD) for GM and/or 
non-operating items.

Downside risks to our PO are: (1) softer AI server demand due to GPU/components 
shortage, (2) GM pressure due to higher raw material cost (e.g. steel, aluminum), (3) 
softer kitchen slides market demand under macro uncertainty/weakness, (4) delayed 
product certification, (5) slower capacity expansion, (6) unfavorable FX movement 
(mainly USD/NTD) to weigh on GM and/or non-operating items.

3Q26 GM slightly lower: Off revenue of NT$120,744mn (+26% QoQ/+66% YoY),
GM contracted by 2.1ppt QoQ to 17.6% (vs. MSe and consensus both at 18.5%) on
higher component costs. With opex of NT$5,162mn (opex ratio 4.3% vs. MSe 4.3%/
consensus  4.0%), OP was NT$16,122mn (OPM 13.4%), 6%/5% lower than MSe of NT
$17,183mn (OPM 14.2%)/consensus  of NT$17,047mn (OPM 14.4%), respectively. With
a narrower non-op gain of NT$94mn, net profit came in at NT$13,074mn (EPS NT

$23.4), 2%/5% short of MSe/consensus of NT$13,328mn (EPS NT$23.9)/NT
$13,748mn (EPS NT$24.5).
Outlook still on optimistic side: We expect Accton to grow revenue sequentially
into 4Q26. Network switch customers will likely continue to pull in more for their AI
clusters amid 800G migration. Meanwhile, its AI accelerator customer should ramp
more of its new generation product and increase adoption of liquid cooling thermal
solutions. To mitigate pressure from elevated component costs, it has negotiated

with customers for them to consign some components, e.g., DRAM, from late 3Q26.
Although this might cap revenue growth upside for Accton, it should help on the
GM side. We model revenue of NT$130,083mn (+8% QoQ/+81% YoY), gross margin
of 20.0% (+2.4ppt QoQ), operating margin of 15.5% (+2.1ppt QoQ)  and EPS of NT
$28.2 (+21% QoQ/+88% YoY). Looking to 2027, we see revenue growth of NT
$526,019mn (+26% YoY) and GM of 20.0%, for EPS NT$112.9 EPS (+31% YoY).

Stay OW: We believe Accton should continue to benefit from networking and
compute demand growth amid AI deployment, reflecting its system integration
capability, customer relationships, and execution. For the longer term,  it will likely
tap into opportunities in rack level solutions, optical-based switches, etc. We lower
our PT to NT$2,250 (20x 2027 P/E) from NT$3,500 (30x 2027 P/E), mostly owing
to more conservative sentiment toward AI names.

arrangements.  Margin expansion in 4Q26 should be a positive
catalyst for the share price in the coming months.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
OW with PT of NT$2,250 (previously NT$3,500)
Price target (base case scenario value) –  NT$2,250 (-36%): Our new price target is based
on 20x our 2027 EPS estimate (30x previously). We lower our target multiple to factor in
recent market sentiment toward AI names. We see our target multiple supported by the
45% operating profit CAGR we project for 2025-28. This implies a PEG of 0.4 vs. its
average of 0.7 over 2019-25, when the stock was trading at a P/E of 22x for an operating

profit CAGR of 32%.
Bull case scenario value – NT$3,350 (-20%): We apply 30x to our base case EPS estimate
for 2027 (36x previously). This scenario reflects stronger demand for network switches in
both data center and enterprise applications as well as more orders for AI accelerator
modules.
Bear case scenario value – NT$1,800 (-39%): We apply 10x to our base case EPS estimate
for 2027 (16x previously). This scenario reflects lukewarm data center and enterprise

capex and continued dominance by merchant GPUs in AI servers.
Downside risks to our positive thesis
• Weaker data center capex
• Slower network switch spec migration
• InfiniBand taking more share in AI networks
• Softer custom AI accelerator growth
• Softer ODM direct business model adoptions

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Update
MPI Corporation  | Asia Pacific
Potential further upside in
2027/28 capacity expansion;
stay OW
MPI hosted its 3Q26 earnings call on November 16. Key takeaways:
There could be further upside in 2027/28 capacity expansion: The company
reiterated that it would add at least 2.5mn pins for MEMS and 0.5mn pins for VPC in
2027. It may raise the numbers after its budget plan is finalized in December. MPI
expects to maintain aggressive capacity expansion in 2028, given strong demand.

2027 guidance appears conservative: The company guided for 2027 revenue to be
up at least 50% YoY with gross margin >60% –   conservative vs. consensus
expectations of 2027 revenue doubling. However, we expect MPI to beat its own
guidance  and raise it gradually, per previous practice of amendment.
In qualification for second insertion 2 customer: The company is  still in
qualification for insertion 2. However, it confirmed it has a second significant

customer; the schedule of the qualification could be similar to the first one. MPI said
it would be the only supplier for the first  customer's  insertion 2 process. CPO could
make up a double-digit share of 2028 total revenue.

Key Takeaways
CPO revenue should come mostly   in 2H27/2028, given the equipment lead time
and customers' verification.
It could make up a double-digit  share of 2028 total revenue.
A further 2027 capacity update is due by the end of the year.

to 3Q26's level.

Stay OW – we cite strong AI/HPC demand,  increasing pin count  for the leading-
edge products, and the capability to pass increasing costs on to customers.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
                        MPI Corporation (6223.TWO)                    
We derive our price target from a residual income model, as we believe this can best capture 
the long-term value. We assume a 9.78% cost of equity (risk-free rate 2.0% and risk premium 
6.0%), a payout ratio of 67%, a medium-term growth rate of 16.0%, and a terminal growth rate 
of 4.0%. Our price target implies 50x/25x 2027e/2028e P/E. We believe its valuation will con-
tinue to climb along with growing earnings. 
                        Risks to Upside                    
n Stronger-than-expected AI demand
n Stronger-than-expected market share gain from peers
n Stronger-than-expected CPO development

Risks to Downside                    
n Slower-than-expected AI demand
n Slower-than-expected market share gain from peers
n Slower-than-expected CPO development

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

Analyst Industry Views

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

 절대 (달러환산) (5.2) (2.7) 0.2

3Q26 영업이익 1.8조, 핵심 하락 원인은 인센티브 
환율효과는 전분기 대비 -5,330억 작용했으나, 기말환율 하락에 따른 판매보증비의 급감 
(6,200억, -9,250억 qoq)으로 상쇄되었다 판단. 인센티브 비용 증가세는 -6,060억원의 영
향을 미쳤으며, 대부분 유럽지역 EV2 판매를 위한 딜러 인센티브에서 발생 추정. 
외생변수는 능력 밖의 일, EV 전략에 대해서는 장기성장에 무게 
중동 분쟁 지속, 환율 하락과 같은 외생변수의 부정적 영향을 제외한다면 단기 영업적 측면
의 실적 악재는 BEV 인센티브 지급기조의 변화가 없을 것이라는 점. 중장기 글로벌 BEV 
시장의 입지 강화를 단기 전사 수익성보다 중시하는 전략을 채택. 
마찬가지로, 4분기가 환율 효과의 본격화 
3Q26과 반대로 qoq 기말환율 대비 낙폭 확대할 평균환율 (QTD 1,338원) 효과로 환관련 
감익 본격화 예상. 즉, 판매보증비의 정상화 (qoq 반등)로 인해 수익성 추가 악화 가능성 존
재. 완성차 판매량의 경우 경쟁사 대비 견조했던 2Q-3Q26의 기조를 유지할 것이라 예상. 
투자의견 BUY 유지, 목표주가 129,000원으로 하향 
목표주가 하향 근거는 산정 기준을 FY27F로 변경함과 동시에 FY27F 자동차사업 NOPAT 
예상치 하향과 현대모비스 지분가치 하락. 밸류에이션상 저점에 도달했다는 의견은 FY27F 
EPS의 yoy 하락으로 설득력 약화. 중장기 BEV 시장 입지 강화에 대한 밸류에이션 프리미
엄 반영은 글로벌 전동화 속도에 달려 있음. 주가 상승 트리거는 1) 외생변수의 개선, 2) 신
차 출시를 통한 상품성 강화, 3) BEV 시장 점유율 확대 이후 점진적 인센티브 축소, 4) 20조
원 수준의 순현금 기반 발생 가능한 사업적 결정.

예상 대비 큰 PT/지역 믹스 악화 영향

▶Analyst 김성래 sr.kim@hanwha.com 3772-7751

Buy (유지) 
목표주가(하향): 210,000원

현재 주가(10/30) 95,600원 
상승여력 ▲119.7% 
시가총액 373,235억원 
발행주식수 390,413천주

주가 추이

3Q26 매출액과 영업이익은 모두 당사 추정치를 크게 하회했습니다. 탁월
한 차량 판매 증가세에도 불구하고 부정적 믹스와 환율/원자재가 상승에 따
른 수익성 악화가 아쉬웠습니다. 매크로 영향 고려한 추정치 하향 및 로봇 
지분가치 할인으로 목표주가 21만원으로 조정합니다. 
큰 폭의 판매 성장에도 수익성 크게 악화 
동사 3Q26 매출액은 31.6조원(+10.1% 이하 YoY), 영업이익 1.8조원
(+24.3%)을 기록하며 추정치를 크게 하회했다. 관세 영향이 본격화되었
던 전년 동기 대비 관세율 차이에 따른 비용 감소(5.3천억원)와 판매보증 
충당부채 전입액 감소(6.7천억원) 등 낮은 기저로 이익이 개선되었으나, 
2023년 생산 정상화 이후 가장 큰 폭의 판매 성장(84.7만대, +7.9%)에도 
불구하고 인센티브와 재료비 상승, 중동 물량 감소와 경제형 EV 판매 확
대에 따른 부정적 믹스로 OPM 5.8%에 그친 실적을 기록했다.  
예상보다 큰 부정적 PT/지역 믹스 영향 
기대를 상회하는 판매량 증가에 따라 물량 효과 통한 수익성 개선을 기
대했으나, 예상 대비 큰 3Q 부정적 믹스 영향에 따라 4Q와 ’27년 실적 
개선 기대 또한 낮출 수밖에 없는 상황이다. 아중동 판매 부진과 유럽 모
델 수익성 저하 영향이 판매 물량 증가 효과보다 크게 작용하고 있다. 인
도, 중남미 대비 고마진 차종 믹스가 좋은 중동의 지정학적 영향이 지속
되고 있고, 유럽은 EV2 등 신차 중심 판매 확대에도 중국 로컬업체들의 
유럽 공세 격화로 시장 점유율을 사수하기 위한 모델 저수익화가 예상 
대비 장기화될 것으로 판단한다. 그러나 고정비 만회를 위해 인센티브 
증가와 가격 효과 축소 영향 속에서도 판매 확대는 불가피한 상황이다. 
부정적 상황 장기화 가능성, 목표주가 210,000원으로 하향 
결국, 수익성 개선을 위해서는 고마진 제품 판매 확대 또는 판매가 견조
하나 저마진 차종 원가를 절감하는 방법밖에 없다. 북미 텔루라이드 
HEV 판매와 스포티지HEV HMGMA 증산이 고마진 물량 증가에 지속
기여할 것이다. 그러나 단기 중동 시장 회복과 차종별 원가절감 효과 확
인이 어렵다는 점에서 고마진 제품 판매 확대 효과는 제한적 전망이다. 
이에, 투자의견 Buy 유지하되, 목표주가 21만원으로 조정한다. 실적 추
정치 조정하여 영업가치를 종전 대비 15.7% 하향하고, 성장주 밸류 디스
카운트에 따른 로봇 지분가치 30% 할인하였다.

[ Compliance Notice ] (공표일: 2026 년 11 월 2 일) 
이 자료는 조사분석 담당자가 객관적 사실에 근거해 작성하였으며, 타인의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 
반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 7

기아 (000270) [한화리서치]

7 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

한화에어로스페이스  
(012450)

3Q26: 그래도 믿는다  Company Brief

3분기 매출 6조 943억원/영업이익 4,520억원 
한화에어로스페이스의 3분기 매출은 6조 943억원/영업이익 4,520억원(OPM 7.4%)로 
컨센서스와 당사 추정치를 모두 대폭 하회하는 실적을 기록하였다. 앞서 실적을 발표한 
한화오션의 실적 쇼크를 감안하더라도, 주력사업인 지상방산 부문의 실적이 매출 1조 
2,455억원/영업이익 217억원(1.7%)로 지난 분기(2조 1,075억원/ 5,330억원/ 25.3%) 
는 물론 전년동기(2조 1,098억원/5,725억원/27.1%)보다도 현저히 감소하였다. 
일회성 요인은 대전사업장 폭발사고에 따른 복구비용 등 300억원, 일부 국내사업 
충당금 160억원 및 환율하락에 따른 효과 등이 있었으나 역시 이를 감안해도 내수와 
수출 모두 실질 이익률은 5%미만으로 추정된다. 지상방산 분기 매출은 1분기(1조 
2,211억)보다 오히려 소폭 증가하였으나 영업이익이 1분기 2,087억원에서 
217억원으로 대폭 감소한 점에 대하여 회사는 프로젝트별 믹스 구성에 따른 낮은 
이익률의 인도분이 반영되었다고 설명하고 있으나, 그럼에도 불구하고 수출과 내수 
이익률 모두 BEP에 가까웠다는 점은 쉽게 이해가 가지는 않는다. 다만 방산기업의 
특성상 대전사업장 사고에 따른 실제 납기 영향 등 회사가 공개할 수 없는 사정이 
있었을 것으로 추측할 뿐이다. 대전사업장은 현재 복구작업이 진행 중이며 27년 
상반기 중에는 가동 정상화할 것으로 예상된다.  
긍정적인 소식도 있다. 폴란드향 천무의 연간 인도대수가 기존 가이던스인 40대에서 
60대로 증가하였으며, 이를 포함하여 3분기에 인도되지 않은 물량의 대부분이 
4분기에 집중될 예정으로, 올해의 4분기 실적 개선폭은 통상적으로 볼 수 있는 
4분기의 계절적 집중도보다 더 커질 것으로 예상한다. 방산기업의 실적은 인도일정에 
따라 크게 변할 수 있는 분기 실적보다 연간 합산이 더 중요하다는 점을 한 번 더 
믿고, 4분기를 기다리는 게 좋겠다.

투자의견 BUY 유지, 목표주가 1,200,000원 하향  
투자의견 BUY를 유지하지만, SOTP기반 목표주가는 기존 1,490,000원에서 
1,200,000원으로 19.5% 하향한다. 비영업가치 중 자회사 한화시스템과 한화오션, 
쎄트렉아이의 지분가치 하락(합산 8.7조원→7.1조원), 그리고 지난 분기 대비 늘어난 
별도 순차입금(3.25조→4.95조원)을 반영하였으며 최근 방산섹터 전반의 밸류 하락을 
감안하여 영업가치 중 지상방산+항공우주의 Target P/E를 기존 35배에서 30배로 
조정하였다. 
3분기는 여러모로 악재가 겹쳤다. 주식시장 전반의 하락에 더해 방산섹터 수주 지연에 
따른 디레이팅, 그리고 납기 지연에 따른 실적 하락 등 나올 수 있는 악재는 다 
나왔다고도 볼 수 있다. 그러나 뒤집어보면 지연중인 수주는 시간의 문제일 뿐 가시화 
가능성이 매우 높으며, 실적 또한 진행률 인식이 아닌 인도기준 인식에 따른 일시적 
현상으로 4분기 이후 복구될 가능성이 크다. 펀더멘털의 유의미한 변화는 없다고 볼 
수 있으며 밸류에이션 매력도가 증가한 현 시점에서 추가 매수를 고려할 때다. 다만, 
대전사업장 사고에 따른 당국의 제재조치가 아직 확정되지 않았음은 잔여 리스크 
요인이다. 동종사의 신규계약 입찰제한 제재조치가 주가에 상당한 영향을 미쳤음을 
고려하면 한화에어로스페이스 또한 제재조치 여부를 지켜본 후 투자할 것을 권한다.

Compliance notice 
당 보고서 공표일 기준으로 해당 기업과 관련하여, 
 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
 당 연구원은 한화에어로스페이스(012450)의 일부 비용을 지원받아 한화에어로스페이스 공장 투어(2026년 10월 1-2)를 다녀왔습니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

Company Report  2026.11.12

상선, 방산, 해양, 엔진 모든 사업 영위하는 전천후 기업 
경쟁사 대비 1) 가장 넓은 사업 영역을 보유, 2) 시가총액 또한 높음. 이러한 특성은 동사의 
사업 포트폴리오 균형 관점에서 장점이나, 반대로 투자 매력의 집중을 일정 부분 희석. 경쟁
사 대비 주가 상승 트리거가 가장 폭넓게 존재하나, 상승 탄력은 그만큼 제한. 
상선: FY27F 매출인식은 FY23-25 수주 물량, 가스선 매출비중 하락 
FY27F 상선부문 매출은 FY23-25 수주 물량이 인식되며 (일부 FY26F 수주), FY25 수주
한 물량이 연간 40% 수준의 가장 높은 매출기여도를 보일 것. 2H27F부터 올해 수주한 일
부 선박의 매출인식 또한 발생하며, 연중 가스선 매출 비중이 하락하고 컨테이너선 매출 비
중이 상승. 
방산: 좋게 보자면 불안하고, 나쁘게 보자면 아쉬운 
MASGA를 국내 야드에서 진행한다는 대전제로 경쟁사와 같은 적정가치를 부여해 왔으나, 
미국 현지 건조와 같은 여러 가능성을 고려하면 다음과 같은 불확실성 존재: 1) 중간지주사
와의 미국내 JV 설립 관련 투자 지분 미정, 2) 신규 조선소 건립시 미국내 수주와 매출까지 
걸리는 시간. 
투자의견 BUY 유지, 다 깎아서 봐도 충분한 상승여력 
상선부문 26.5조, 방산 8조, 선박엔진 9조, 발전엔진 (FY30F 기준) 13.5조 수준의 SOTP 
밸류에이션 기반하여 목표주가 643,000원 제시. 발전엔진 증설로 인한 미래가치를 반영하
나, 상선부문 Target P/O multiple 하향 (0.7x, 업종 공통)과 MASGA 적정가치 하향으로 
목표주가 자체는 하락. 보수적 멀티플 제시함에도 주가 상승 여력은 충분히 확보된 상황. 업
종 전체적 긍정요인 외의 동사 주가 상승 트리거는 1) 미국 현지 조선소 투자주체 발탁, 2) 
DC향 발전엔진 추가 수주, 3) MASGA 국내 건조 물량 수주.

Document 309: Cliff Notes_ the source and persistence of inflation (2026-11-21)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ the source and persistence of inflation (2026-11-21).pdf

Date: 2026-11-21

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: the source and persistence of inflation
By Ryan Wells, Illiana Jain & Elliot Clarke
11:16 October 30 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia this week, all eyes were on the September CPI. Headline inflation rose 0.8% (4.3%yr) in
the month, largely owing to a 9.4% jump in fuel prices and a seasonal surge in domestic and
international holiday travel & accommodation. The underlying inflation pulse also proved hotter than

anticipated for Q3 overall, up 1.0% (3.7%yr).

### Page 2

While some parts of the basket are seeing disinflation – housing in particular looks to be cooling faster
than expected as builders lure sales with discounts – other components are still showing persistence.
Highlighting this is market services ex volatiles, which the RBA watches closely as an indicator of price
pressures related to domestic capacity. In Q3, this metric rose 1.2% (3.2%yr), and the monthly detail
points to momentum holding up – in line with our expectations for another chunky read in Q4.

With inflation running well above the RBA’s August forecasts, and some of the energy risks flagged
then materialising, we continue to expect the Monetary Policy Board will deliver a 25bp rate hike to
4.85% in November. Beyond November, another rate hike in February is a risk, but is not currently part
of our base case.
Across the Tasman, the RBNZ this week surprised market expectations for a 25bp rate hike, deciding
instead to wait for further information before taking the next step with policy. The decision was made

4:3, with the Governor in the ‘on hold’ camp. A 25bp hike in December to 3.00% and further tightening
in 2027 (Westpac NZ forecasts a peak of 4.00% in September) seems probable; however, each
decision will depend on fuel prices, inflation expectations and evidence of second-round pricing
pressures, along with the evolution of global economic risks.
In the US, the FOMC also held rates steady at its October meeting. Based on prior comments by key
members, the decision was not a surprise, and neither were Governor Cook and Cleveland Fed

President Hammack’s dissents in favour of a 25bp hike. The Committee remains positive on economic
activity, characterising it in the statement as “expanding at a strong pace” (an upgrade from “solid” in
September). Chair Warsh also subsequently emphasised the breadth of economic growth outside of
tech-related investment (evinced by Q3 GDP the day after; see below).
The Committee refrained from spotlighting key price risks in the statement. However, inflation “remains

elevated” and the FOMC are determined to achieve a “timely” and sustainable return of annual inflation
to 2.0%yr after 6 years above target. This guidance and the “accumulation of more information will help
the Committee determine the policy stance” suggests another 25bp hike is likely in December.
Thereafter, the FOMC will remain attentive to any broadening in inflation and related risks through
2027; although, in the absence of another shock, we expect them to remain on hold.

The Q3 GDP report certainly supported Chair Warsh’s view on the breadth of growth, the 3.4%
annualised gain for GDP benefitting from a 3.8% annualised increase in household consumption,
12.7% growth in non-residential investment as well as a robust increase in government spending.
Notably though, the contribution from business investment was effectively offset by net exports’ drag
given most of the componentry related to AI infrastructure investment is imported. This is not to say that

US firms won’t benefit from the investment once complete, but simply that much of the initial return
goes to the foreign businesses who provide the necessary capital equipment.
On consumption, the monthly PCE data implies downside risks remain for the consumer, the increase
in nominal spending seen in the month requiring dissaving and, on a real basis, consumption flat in the
month following a 0.5% gain in August. Cost-of-living pressures and uncertainty over the outlook is

### Page 3

Related articles
expected to see household consumption growth average around trend over the coming year, albeit with
considerable volatility quarter to quarter. This should see PCE inflation slow back towards its 2.0%yr
medium-term target as the FOMC desires, from 3.6%yr and 3.1%yr for the headline and core
measures respectively as of September.
Across the Atlantic, the ECB also left its key policy rates unchanged in October. Of note from the
meeting communications, energy prices continue to pose upside risks for inflation, but also downside

risks for growth. "While the full effects of the energy shock are still unfolding, underlying inflation has
remained broadly stable so far and wages do not show a material response". "Financing conditions
have [also] tightened", "which is likely to weigh on inflation and growth". In the press conference,
President Lagarde noted that, between the October and December meetings, a significant amount of
new economic information will be released, allowing forecasts and risks assessments to be tested.

Based on the October meeting communications, we continue to expect a rate hike at the December
meeting but believe this will be prove to be the last hike of the cycle. Note though, the spectrum of risks
Europe faces is broad and skewed towards inflation in the near term, hence the Council’s guidance will
remain hawkish. President Lagarde also announced today that she will step down as ECB President in
February ahead of the scheduled conclusion of her term next October.

Document 343: Germany’s hidden advantage in the AI race

Source PDF: all report pdf/Germany’s hidden advantage in the AI race.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 4

### Page 1

Berlin may be Germany’s innovation powerhouse, but across
Europe, the country is still playing catch-up. When it comes to
AI, however, it could have much more up its sleeve than its
overall innovation performance suggests

Franziska Biehl and Carsten Brzeski
Once we turn from traditional innovation to artiﬁcial intelligence, Germany
suddenly looks much more competitive
Germany’s innovation problem runs deep…
Germany’s innovation landscape remains highly fragmented. Berlin
tops our national Innovation Index for the 12th consecutive year,
followed by Hamburg and Baden-Württemberg, while Thuringia,
Mecklenburg-Western Pomerania and Saxony-Anhalt remain at the
bottom, held back by demographic pressures, skills shortages and
weak business creation.
These regional diﬀerences reﬂect the structural challenges holding
back innovation capacity across the country. Consequently,
Germany only ranks 16th out of the 21 eurozone countries in our
ING Germany Innovation Index for the eurozone, leaving one of
Your privacy is important

Targeting Cookies
Save preferences Accept all

### Page 2

Europe’s largest economies in the lower half of the table. Ireland
remains the eurozone’s innovation leader, followed by Luxembourg
and Estonia, while Bulgaria, Greece and Italy sit at the bottom.
At ﬁrst sight, Germany's low position in our innovation ranking isn't
good news for its economic prospects. However, in the age of AI,
inventing new technologies is increasingly becoming a smaller part
of the equation. The winners might be those able to adopt,

integrate and put AI applications to productive use quickly. And
once we turn from traditional innovation to artiﬁcial intelligence,
Germany suddenly looks much more competitive.
…but on AI, it jumps ahead
Our new ING Germany AI Index, covering research, investment,
patents, adoption and ICT skills, puts Germany in sixth place,
suggesting the country already has many of the ingredients
needed to turn AI’s potential into economic gains.
The countries at the top of our ranking have a clear advantage

when it comes to research. Finland recorded more than six high-
impact AI publications per capita, while Luxembourg produced
more than 10. Germany managed only 3.5. The gap in funding is
even larger: relative to GDP, Finland invested around ﬁve times as
much in AI start-ups as Germany.
But digital talent could matter just as much. The AI race isn't only
about developing models; it's also about having enough people
who know what to do with them.
Digital talent and AI adoption

Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.
You can review and change your preferences at any time in
our Cookie policy.
Essential
Performance Cookies
Targeting Cookies

### Page 3

Source: Eurostat
In Germany, more than 5% of employees work in ICT-related jobs
and roughly 6% of university graduates complete their studies in
an ICT-related ﬁeld. Around one in four German businesses already
uses AI, and Germany is among the leading locations for AI-related
patents.
The same pattern emerges elsewhere in Europe. In Luxembourg,
Finland and the Netherlands, between 7% and 9% of employees
work in ICT-related jobs. At the same time, these countries are

among Europe’s frontrunners in AI adoption, with around one in
three businesses and one in four individuals already using AI.
Greece sits at the other end of the spectrum: its pool of ICT
specialists is much smaller, and fewer than one in ten businesses
use AI.
Digital talent could be Germany’s AI edge
Germany's comparative strength may lie less in developing its own
large AI models and more in applying them across its industrial
base and providing the infrastructure to do so. Investment plans are

moving in that direction too. The German government plans to at
least double data centre capacity by 2030 and quadruple available
AI computing capacity. International technology companies,
including Nvidia and Amazon, are also investing in German AI
computing and cloud infrastructure.
Building innovative capacity takes years, if not decades. But

We have published our 2027 Macro Outlook: Steady Baseline, Downside Risks. We flesh 
out the Global Market implications and key asset views here, highlighting 10 
investment themes that are likely to dominate price action in 2027. More detailed 
outlooks for the individual asset classes are forthcoming.  
A maturing market cycle: Stable global growth and declining inflation make for 1.
a friendly macro baseline, but risks are to the downside as the market cycle ages. 
The AI investment impulse peaks: AI has contributed more to market gains 2.
than economic growth, so a bigger test for markets than the economy.  
Passing—or dropping—the baton: Equity gains move from AI infrastructure to 3.
other areas, but we see risks if tighter financial conditions thwart the transition 
or markets look further ahead. 
Energy—balancing risk and risk premium: Our baseline is for a gradual decline 4.
in energy prices, but an escalation-driven spike remains one of the largest 
near-term risks to markets.

Goldman Sachs Global Markets Outlook 2027

### Page 3

Markets Outlook 2027: Growing Pains

1. A maturing market cycle 
Stable growth, declining inflation make for a friendly macro backdrop. n
But risks are to the downside, and an ageing market cycle is facing more headwinds. n
Higher rates, wider spreads, and a peaking AI impulse mean the broad asset picture n
is more two-sided ... 
... and argues for more diversified allocations, including bonds and hedges. n
Our headline macro forecasts for 2027 are quite friendly: stable growth, declining 
inflation and peaking policy rates. But as the economic cycle extends, the market cycle is 
increasingly showing its age. For 2026, our view was that even though asset market 
valuations were running ahead of the macro, a positive cyclical backdrop including 
strong investment growth should dominate. While the Iran war has upended many 
forecasts, even without the energy shock the market is running into more conventional 
late-cycle headwinds. Real rates are higher, credit spreads are wider and volatility is 
picking up, especially under the surface. On top of this, the AI impulse, which has been a 
key support to markets through the shocks this year, looks poised to peak in the coming 
year. 
That is likely to prove a bigger headwind to the profit and asset market picture than 
growth, given its larger role in supporting equity markets. Markets have already moved 
some way towards reflecting that the extraordinary surge in profitability in the AI 
infrastructure complex is unlikely to be extrapolated. So a flattening in the gains there 
may be offset by gains further down the waterfall among AI users or other cyclical parts 
of the market, especially if energy prices and rates fall more. But that is a tricky 
balancing act, and further boosts to equity prices are likely to come alongside a higher 
base level of volatility. There are also risks that AI-related drawdowns tighten financial 
conditions more meaningfully, or that energy and policy relief do not arrive on time. 
That leaves the broader risk-asset picture looking more two-sided than it has for several 
years, arguing for more diversified and better-hedged portfolios. 
12 November 2026   3
Goldman Sachs Global Markets Outlook 2027

### Page 4

2. The AI investment impulse peaks 
The AI investment impulse peak is a bigger test for markets than the economy. n
That impulse has been a larger contributor to growth in market value and earnings n
than in GDP. 
Markets have already de-rated AI infrastructure beneficiaries. n
Valuation excesses are less visible, so the focus is on sustainability of record profits. n
Coming into 2026, we expected continued acceleration in AI investment to dominate 
valuation concerns in equities, but likely amid higher volatility and wider credit spreads.  
Although we expect solid AI investment growth to continue, our forecasts imply that the 
investment growth impulse—additional AI investment as a share of GDP—will peak in 
early 2027. From a macro perspective, this should be a manageable event. But it may be 
a bigger test for markets for three reasons. First, the AI impulse has been a much larger 
contributor to growth in earnings and market value than to growth in GDP. Second, 
AI-related equities have taken credit for significant macro productivity gains, especially 
companies benefiting directly from the infrastructure boom, that have made them more 
vulnerable to any signs that we are on a less optimistic path. Third, the point at which 
growth rates peak (a “second derivative” turning point) often matters, even if underlying 
growth rates (the “first derivative”) remain healthy. Moving through that peak allows 
markets to place limits on the upside of an uncertain distribution and, as growth 
decelerates, the period of positive surprises to expectations often ends too, as we may 
be starting to see for the AI capex cycle. 
Markets have already been reflecting these concerns. Since June, we have seen a 
significant de-rating of companies levered to the AI infrastructure theme, which are 
most sensitive to shifts in the investment outlook and had been the biggest market 
beneficiaries in the first half of 2026. Moreover, robust profits, and a rising share of

income going to listed companies, have helped keep both the imbalances and the deep 
valuation excesses that we saw in the late 1990s at bay. The key question in this cycle is 
whether the market is correctly assessing how much of the recent acceleration in profits, 
which has taken S&P 500 earnings to a record high relative to GDP, will prove persistent, 
especially as the investment cycle itself fades. Without the capital inflows from the EM 
crises in 1997 and 1998—or the rate cuts that goods price deflation allowed during that 
tech cycle—increased financing needs have also been putting clearer upward pressure 
on the cost of capital.

3. Passing—or dropping—the baton 
Strong levels of AI investment growth and profitability should limit the pressure. n
And the baton may be passed from AI infrastructure to other market areas. n
But we see risks of a larger aggregate shock if the market looks forward, or other n
areas cannot take up the slack. 
Tighter financial conditions or credit conditions could create a feedback loop. n
Two things give us comfort in terms of how bumpy this transition through the peak of 
the investment cycle will prove to be. The first is that although the investment impulse 
from AI looks poised to peak, growth in both AI investment and earnings should remain 
firmly positive in 2027, especially earlier in the year. We expect S&P 500 earnings growth 
to decelerate from 36% in 2026 to a still-robust 11% in 2027. This may ultimately help to 
limit the de-rating of even the most directly affected areas and should reduce the 
impact on broader growth-sensitive assets, though it should also imply less relief in core 
rates pricing. The second is that the baton may be passed, at least to a degree, from the 
direct beneficiaries of the AI investment boom to other areas of the market. This 
includes other areas of the AI complex, such as the hyperscalers, if monetization paths 
become more visible; companies that may begin to show benefits from AI productivity 
enhancements; or non-AI companies that see some relief as the crowding out of capital

available to them eases. 
The risk is that what could be more of a rotation—and a flattening in the upward trend in 
asset markets—becomes a bigger aggregate shock. This could happen because the 
market begins to look further forward to the peak in the investment share itself (in 2028 
on our forecasts); because the passing of the baton to other sectors does not take place 
as quickly or as smoothly as needed; or because feedback loops from financial and 
credit conditions accelerate more quickly than in our baseline. We think the risk to 
markets is higher than the risk to the economic picture on each front, because the boom 
itself has had its largest impact there. For that reason, the most likely channel through 
which AI worries could lead to broader economic downside is through financial 
conditions themselves.

4. Energy—balancing risk and risk premium 
An escalation-driven oil spike remains one of the largest near-term risks to markets. n
Our baseline is for a gradual decline in energy prices, but the hedge value of energy n
is still high. 
Downside tail to energy prices with clearer/faster resolution. n
Risk premium has fluctuated, so hedge value also varies over time. n
A potential oil price spike driven by another escalation in the war with Iran remains one 
of the biggest near-term risks to the markets and growth picture. Particularly after the 
fall in oil prices so far in November, pricing is vulnerable to any broadening in the conflict 
and new supply disruptions. We would expect that to hurt equities, support the Dollar 
against European currencies and put upward pressure on rates, all else equal. Europe, 
too, faces a precarious winter, given low storage levels in natural gas which leave the 
region vulnerable to risks from both supply and weather. If those risks are avoided, our 
baseline is for a gradual decline in energy prices over the coming year even without full 
resolution of the conflict. Despite the ongoing war with Iran, oil market supply has 
largely been restored (even if refining capacity is limited), but risks of escalation and 
further disruptions have generally led the market to demand a fluctuating but 
meaningful risk premium above where we think the fundamental supply-demand picture 
would leave them.

### Page 7

Our baseline is a gradual recovery in supply and reduction in energy prices, leaving 
energy and core commodity returns more muted. But the risk of more serious 
disruptions is real, and energy prices could move significantly higher in those scenarios 
at a time when equities and, potentially, bonds could both be under pressure. The 
hedging value of the asset class remains high for that reason. The wrinkle is that the risk 
premium in oil has varied substantially and conditions the value of the hedge. When the 
risk premium falls, as it has done more recently, that hedging value is likely to be higher. 
When that risk premium is very high, as it was at the end of October, oil markets are 
more vulnerable to downside tails if news flow is better than expected, and outright 
longs (rather than options) have generally been more risky. The downside tail from faster 
progress towards resolution than we expect (not just in Iran, but in Ukraine too) is one of 
the clearest sources of upside risks for broader markets. In that scenario, energy prices 
could come down more quickly, supporting both bonds and equities.

5. Bonds reclaim macro and hedging value 
Rates moved up in 2026, on oil, hikes, AI spending and fiscal deficits. n
Central bank pricing now too hawkish relative to our baseline. n
Bonds should be a better hedge as supply shocks give way to demand stresses. n
Higher running yield, more paths to relief, and cheap valuations argue for higher n
bond allocations. 
Interest rates moved higher in 2026 after a low point on the eve of the Iran war. The 
resultant surge in energy prices and central bank pricing has been the primary catalyst 
across most DM and EM rates markets, but real rates, especially in the US, have 
continued to move higher as investment demand from the AI build-out interacts with 
sustained fiscal deficits. Despite that backdrop, we think that central bank pricing in 
many economies, including the US and the UK, is too hawkish relative to what is likely to 
be delivered. Even with the energy shock, the underlying inflation story is still fairly 
benign, which means that policy rates are likely to peak at lower levels relative to 
2023-2024 and reverse earlier. The risk to this view is that the energy shock extends 
further or inflation remains stickier and the labour market proves more resilient, as it did 
this year. 
Bonds have been a poor hedge in this environment of energy supply shocks, rate hikes

### Page 8

and real rate pressures from private and public sector spending. But we think the case 
for duration has shifted in a more constructive direction for several reasons. First, on a 
static basis, bond yields are now providing a good running yield and a decent spread to 
cash. Second, valuations are cheap (we see UST 10y yields at 4.6% by end-2027), 
positioning is underweight, and there are paths to some shallow rate relief from current 
levels under our benign baseline scenario, and scope for larger moves if AI spending 
decelerates more quickly. Third, the increase in yields has started to create stresses that 
indicate that a higher level of real rates may be difficult to sustain. Bonds are already 
starting to illustrate their value as a hedge against shocks of these types—with Bunds 
rallying as French sovereign spreads widened and USTs rallying as tech stocks came 
under pressure. Although energy spikes remain a near-term risk, we are more positive 
about bond allocations even after the recent rally, in part because of that hedging value.

6. A more cyclical Dollar, Asia’s revaluing currencies 
After tight ranges in 2026, lower inflation, energy prices make for a softer Dollar. n
But a bigger USD swing could come if US tech exceptionalism erodes. n
JPY is a more attractive hedge with ‘team Japan’ focused on limiting Yen weakness. n
CNY appreciation has further to run given undervaluation and a surging trade n
surplus.  
The broad Dollar is finishing 2026 on the strong side of tight ranges, buoyed by a sturdy 
US nominal growth backdrop, higher energy prices, and a shift to more hawkish Fed 
pricing. Under our baseline forecast, all three impulses should fade: alongside trend 
growth we expect inflation in the US to converge towards target, energy prices should 
decline as the Middle East disruptions gradually normalize and we expect the Fed to 
move from hikes to cuts as we head into late 2027 and early 2028. That should make for

### Page 9

a softer Dollar backdrop, and we have pencilled in a roughly 3% fall in the broad 
trade-weighted Dollar in 2027. 
But the real swing factor for FX is likely to be how the peak in AI-related tech spending 
affects US equity returns. Over the past several years, tech-related macro and market 
outperformance in the US has sucked in equity capital flows from the rest of the world 
and has been the primary factor supporting the Dollar’s premium valuation. Our 
baseline forecasts envisage a more even distribution of returns globally rather than US 
underperformance. But if US tech performance erodes meaningfully, that would likely 
come alongside some cyclical underperformance of the Dollar rather than a traditional 
‘safe haven’ bid, as we have already seen in short periods over the past couple of years. 
Instead, we would expect the undervalued Yen to prove a better haven in that scenario, 
given its typical sensitivity to equities and rates falling together. With Japanese policy 
authorities also now focused on limiting further Yen depreciation and encouraging 
inflows into Japanese assets, we think that the Yen should increasingly be a more 
attractive hedge to pro-cyclical portfolios. We also see scope for the Yuan to continue to 
appreciate gradually as China’s trade surplus keeps growing, and forward markets 
underestimate how far this process can run given the deep undervaluation starting 
point. While domestic demand is also very weak, we have argued that China needs both 
currency appreciation and expansionary policy, two instruments to hit two targets, to 
move towards better external and internal balance.

7. Europe & UK—global shocks and local shifts 
European markets have been under pressure from energy ToT, higher global yields n
and sovereign stresses. 
Even if global shocks abate, local concerns still persist until the French election, n
keeping EUR downside attractive.

### Page 10

UK equities are resilient and defensive, Sterling has continued to strengthen. n
UK Gilts may be clearer outperformers given their highest beta to oil prices. n
European macro markets have had a tough year, primarily reflecting a negative terms of
trade shock, rate tightening without an offsetting investment spending boom, and most
recently the reappearance of sovereign stresses, especially in France. Even with the
repricing, there are still challenges: the energy risks are likely to be most acute going

through the winter, and the underlying fiscal issues in France are unlikely to be
adequately addressed until the French election in the Spring of 2027. As a result, using
the Euro as a funder for other crosses has been a popular position, and the skew of risks
and low carry continue to make downside optionality attractive for the near term. While
Europe remains hostage to global shocks, the main paths to relief also run through those
global channels—energy relief would be most helpful, as would lower yields in the rest of

core markets. Together they would also lessen the immediate pressure on fiscal budgets,
even though the underlying primary deficit would still need to be addressed. Any
slowdown in AI-related spending may also show European assets in a better relative light
given a much smaller share of tech in the region’s equity indices.
In those scenarios, the UK may shine even brighter. Gilts have had the highest beta to oil
prices, so a reduction in those pressures may provide the most relief to UK rates and to

the fiscal picture there. UK equity indices also have less tech exposure than the US
(although they do embed significant energy exposure), and so may also prove more
defensive in an AI-related spending slowdown and any associated market pressures. And
while the currency may also benefit from a softer Dollar backdrop, it is already the most
overvalued currency within our G10 coverage. Much has gone right for the Pound in
recent months—a smooth transition to a new Labour government, a ‘do-no-harm’

budget delivery, the BoE shifting to hikes, and encouraging talk of a closer settlement
with the EU. But we think further gains are likely to be more challenging, with our
monetary policy forecasts most dovish to market pricing in the UK.
12 November 2026   10
Goldman Sachs Global Markets Outlook 2027

### Page 11

8. The stress of financing the boom 
AI investment financing has driven credit and equity issuance to cycle records. n
This has been digestible so far, but with discounted pricing needed in credit that has n
led spreads wider. 
A further widening in US spreads looks likely and a higher chance of meaningful n
widening than meaningful tightening. 
Europe offers more risk premium; mortgages and structured credit give some refuge n
from corporate supply. 
Credit and equity issuance has been hitting fresh cycle records, and the financing needs 
of the AI investment boom mean that those needs are likely to build further in 2027. So 
far, that issuance has—for the most part—been digestible, albeit with discounted 
pricing, and the credit underperformance that we expected in 2026 because of these 
dynamics has only really become clear in the last few months. Balance sheets of the 
major borrowers are strong and while free cash flow positions for the hyperscalers have 
deteriorated sharply, the overall corporate sector is much more balanced. We expect 
continued bifurcation between AI-related issuers and “everything else”, with episodic 
indigestion of jumbo AI debt financing leading to wider spreads for AI-related sectors.  
We expect further widening in index-level credit spreads in 2027, though in our base 
case the scope is fairly modest. The risks around that view are skewed though. It is hard 
to see scenarios where US credit spreads tighten sharply (there is more scope for 
tightening in Europe where the risk premium has increased more), and easier to see how 
spreads could move wider, particularly if economic conditions deteriorate more than we 
expect or if investors become more uncomfortable about the profitability of the AI 
investment boom. We expect modest US HY underperformance (decompression) in our

### Page 12

baseline, but the risk is that problems in the left tail of weaker borrowers create more 
turbulent conditions for broader financing. Mortgages and structured credit markets 
may offer some refuge from the heavy corporate supply. From 1998 to 2000, there was 
sustained widening in credit spreads and substantial decoupling between credit and 
equity markets. Without the large capital inflows and goods price deflation from the EM 
crises in 1997 and 1998, we think that kind of divergence will be harder now.

9. EM—resilience and diversification 
2026 is one more year when EM assets have shown resilience/outperformance in the n
face of external shocks. 
But the flip side is less compelling valuations and lower carry, despite friendly macro. n
So the case for EM investing is exposure to high, diversified and resilient yields in EM n
fixed income ... 
... and a regional and sectorally diversified earnings mix in EM equities.  n
EM assets have again demonstrated impressive resilience in 2026 in the face of shocks 
from high energy prices and the spikes in core rates. EM rates have outperformed core 
rates, EM FX total returns are outpacing G9 and EM equities are on track for a narrower 
but still double-digit year after 30% gains in 2025. The upshot of this resilience and 
outperformance is that in many places valuations are less compelling than before. EM 
local rate spreads are tight relative to core rates, EM credit spreads are the tightest in 
years despite attractive all-in yields, and EM FX carry and valuations are firmly 
middle-of-the-road. 
Even though the baseline macro backdrop looks friendly for EM investing and 
carry-harvesting in 2027, less attractive valuations and carry levels mean that it is about 
gaining exposure to high diversified yields in both hard and local currency fixed income. 
In many cases, such as South Africa, Hungary, Colombia and India, those higher yields 
come with improving fiscal and structural reform stories. Likewise, in the case of EM 
equities, while the major markets are not expensive, much of the value in the headline 
index is an artefact of the surge in AI earnings that will be hard to repeat. Rather, the 
case for EM equity length is also about gaining exposure to a diversified earnings mix.

The EM equity index, especially the less concentrated, equal-weighted flavours, provide 
exposure not just to the tech and AI complex in North Asia, but also to cyclical and 
commodity-related growth in South Africa and LatAm, and to larger domestic demand 
stories such as India. And if we see a larger move lower in core rates or the Dollar than 
our baseline expectations, EM local assets provide upside convexity that is harder to 
replicate elsewhere.

10. Growth risks and tail hedges 
A benign baseline, but growth risks now skew to the downside. n
Diversification benefits from bonds, commodities and equity selection. n
Equity index volatility has scope to rise further. n
Downside in rates, USD/JPY, upside in energy and protection in equity options. n
With a steady economic backdrop, our baseline forecasts for 2027 are for positive 
returns across equities and bonds. But with a maturing market cycle and significant 
uncertainty around core drivers of the outlook, we continue to emphasize the value of 
diversification and hedges to protect against key risks. Some of those risks—from higher 
oil prices or sticky core inflation—are likely to push rates higher. But a growing number—
a sharper reversal in the AI boom, cybersecurity fears, AI-related labour-shedding or 
financial stresses—would push both growth and rate expectations lower. The further we 
move into 2027, the more likely it is that the key risks are positive for bonds, as the 
threats from central bank hikes, energy price spikes and the AI investment tailwind 
recede. Diversification remains valuable in this environment. As we have said, the value 
of bonds as a diversifying asset is returning as the skew of risks shifts. And 
commodities—especially energy—continue to play a hedging role for portfolios, 
particularly when implied risk premia fall as they have recently.  
Alongside diversification, direct hedges are worth considering. Positioning for lower 
rates in the US and Japan, or for jointly lower rates and equities are potential ways to 
protect against deeper downside growth tails. In FX, USD/JPY downside may play a 
similar role, given that it generally performs well when rates and equities are both falling

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
All state Housing Sentiment Indexes are at weak levels.
However, November saw a bigger decline in Western
Australia (–4.8%mth) which now has the weakest housing-
related sentiment index across the major states at just 80.9.
Queensland and South Australia moved in line with the

national index. Moves were notably milder in New South
Wales (–1.7%) and especially Victoria (–0.7%) where the state
index, at 86.6, is the least pessimistic of the major states.
The sub-group detail showed a more pronounced weakening in
housing-related sentiment amongst high income earners and
consumers aged over 55 with sentiment holding about steady
amongst low-income earners, albeit at weaker overall levels.
Our November consumer sentiment survey also included

two new regular questions on consumers’ property buying
and selling intentions over the next twelve months. These
questions will continue to be run every month.
Despite the broader pessimism around housing, a relatively
high number of consumers are planning to buy in 2027.
Responses show 12.8% of consumers plan to buy a new
home over the next year. Comparable figures from Westpac’s
Home Ownership survey going back to 2019 suggest this is a
relatively firm result, up slightly since the middle of the year,

on par with the start of 2025 and above the levels reported
during the COVID pandemic in 2020-21. The result suggests
there is significant ‘latent demand’ for home purchases in
Australia, and that many buyers are just waiting for the right
opportunity. The state detail shows intentions are particularly
high in Queensland where one in five consumers is reportedly
planning to buy in 2027.
Interestingly, the positive intentions are not just around home

purchase with 11.2% of consumers reporting that they plan
to buy an investment property over the next year. Again,
comparable figures from Westpac’s Home Ownership survey
show this is up a tick compared to May and only marginally
below the 11.5% reporting intentions to buy investment
properties back in January 2025.
BULLETIN
The Westpac Housing Sentiment Index fell 2.2% to 83.2 in
November from 85.3 in October.
The Index is a composite based on housing-related sentiment

measures from the Westpac-Melbourne Institute Consumer
Sentiment survey. It already appears regularly in Westpac’s
Housing Pulse reports but will now be published on a
monthly basis. The Index has proven to be an extremely useful
predictor of turning points in housing markets, both nationally
and across states. Westpac is also introducing two new
survey questions on consumers property buying and selling
intentions that will be run every month.
The November update shows housing-related sentiment

remains remain deeply pessimistic. At 85.3, the latest Index
read is in the bottom 3% of observations since 2005, when all
components first became available. That said, it is still above the
81.4 low in late 2022 and the 78.6 registered when the COVID
pandemic hit in 2020. Housing-related sentiment was markedly
weaker during the slump in the late 1980s/early 1990s.
Component-wise, the November sentiment decline centred on
a sharp downgrade to price expectations. As detailed below,

the Westpac Housing Sentiment Index composite is based
on four sub-indexes: one tracking assessments of ‘time to buy
a dwelling’; one tracking ‘consumer house price expectations’;
one tracking consumer expectations for unemployment and a
measure of risk aversion based on consumer assessments of
the ‘wisest place for savings’.
Consumer price expectations took a big step down in
November. The Westpac–Melbourne Institute Index of
House Price Expectations recorded a steep 13.4% drop

to 99.6, marking the second biggest monthly deterioration
since the COVID period and the first net negative (sub-100)
read since November 2022. The detailed responses show
consumers are now evenly split on the outlook, just over 38%
expecting more declines, 19% expecting no change and 38%
expecting a rise (5% reported “don’t know).
At least some consumers are starting to see lower prices as
a potential buying opportunity. The ‘time to buy a dwelling’

index rose 1.3% to 89.6 in November, to be up 1.3% on a year
ago as well but still at a very weak level overall, well below
the long run average of 114.
The wider economic backdrop was also less conducive for
housing-related sentiment. Consumers continue to report
a gradual rise in job-loss fears and risk aversion remains
elevated near historic highs. Both factors are likely to be
deterring prospective home buyers.
Housing sentiment sinks but buying intentions positive

“Despite the broader pessimism
around housing, a relatively high
number of consumers are planning
to buy ...”
Matthew Hassan
Head of Australian Macro-Forecasting

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Conversely, just 8.2% of Australians are planning to sell a home 
over the next year. Comparable figures from Westpac’s Home 
Ownership survey suggest this is down significantly from 10.3% 
back in May and near the 7.7% low recorded at the start of 2024. 
On investment properties, 6.7% of all those surveyed reported 
plans to sell. However, only 23% of consumers reported 
owning an investment property – as a share of this sub-
group, the proportion planning to sell is just over 26%. While 
it is unclear whether this is relatively high (no comparable 
measures are available from Westpac’s Home Ownership 
survey), a third of this same sub-group reported an intention to 
buy an investment property over the next year, indicating that 
amongst investment property owners prospective buyers still 
outnumber prospective sellers. 
Overall, the consumer views on housing show a clear tension. 
On the one side, high interest rates, a continued slide in prices 
and unease about a riskier outlook for jobs and the wider 
economy are weighing heavily on housing-related sentiment. 
But against this, relatively few are planning to sell and there 
is a relatively high proportion of consumers looking to buy. 
This suggests that conditions could recover quite quickly if and 
when the macro-economic backdrop becomes less threatening 
and the market starts to stabilise.

The survey is conducted by OZINFO & DYNATA. Respondents are selected at random. This latest survey is based on 1200 adults aged 18 years and over, across Australia. It was conducted in the week from 
2 November to 6 November.  The data have been weighted to reflect Australia’s population distribution. Copyright at all times remains with the Melbourne Institute of Applied Economic and Social Research.
About the Westpac Housing Sentiment Index 
The Westpac Housing Sentiment Index is a composite 
measure based on a weighted combination of four indexes 
from the Westpac–Melbourne Institute Consumer 
Sentiment survey. Two of these are ‘primary’ components 
with a higher weight that relate directly to consumer 
perceptions of housing market conditions: the Westpac–
Melbourne Institute ‘time to buy a dwelling’ index and the 
Westpac–Melbourne Institute House Price Expectations 
Index. The remaining ‘supplementary’ components, with 
lower weights, relate to consumer assessments of job 
security – the Westpac–Melbourne Institute Unemployment 
Expectations Index – and risk appetite – the Westpac Risk 
Aversion Index.
Each of these components is seasonally adjusted, converted 
to a consistent base and combined using fixed weights 
determined by historical regression analysis. Note that the 
house price expectations component is only available from 
2009 on – a re-weighted composite based on the remaining 
measures is used for earlier periods.  
The resulting composite measures provide significant insight 
into housing market conditions both nationally and at the 
individual state level. The national index has over 50yrs of 
history and a clear lead indicator relationship with a variety 
of housing market metrics. The index is particularly good at 
picking turning points in housing market turnover – correctly 
anticipating every major upswing and downturn since 1980 
with a lead of around three months (four once the timeliness 
of sentiment updates is included).
